## [2.8.3] - 2026-09-28

2.8 cut 4 — **the skill "activation gate" gets an input surface** (刀 B, touches `src/`; a *non-content* cut ⇒ hard anchor = B10 verbatim, **but** gates carry gameplay meaning ⇒ all three gates of the gameplay-knife protocol fired, including the independent recompute). Since v2.7.3 the engine could *evaluate* a gate; no user could *write* one. This cut connects that pipe end to end and, in doing so, establishes the project's strictest rule about reading what the user typed.

- **Requirements, settled before code** (user's ruling 2026-09-27, three forks resolved 2026-09-28): (a) **plan A** — Excel and the editor header hold only **base values**; a panel value = base + skill bonus is **derived by the engine**, so there is no "hand-type the panel number" surface (recorded as policy this round; the derivation channel belongs to a future "value change + duration" cut); (b) **plan A + line break** — several "if/then" branches in one cell are separated **by the user's own newline**, and the parser splits on newline; (c) "my player's hand count" = the hand count of **the player seat that owns the skill**, with the standing declaration **"in my game only players hold cards — generals never do"** (promoted into §4 frozen rules). Carried-over wording: gates attach **only to a specific effect**, "choose one of" is out of scope this round, and **in Excel a gate is one column of plain-language text with conditions separated by 、 or ,**.
- **Four places wired**: data layer `SkillEffect.conditions?` (the type lives in `src/data/generals.ts`; `src/skills/dataTypes.ts` only re-exports it, keeping the skills→data arrow one-way); text↔structure translation = new `src/skills/skillGateText.ts` (one grammar `[subject?] metric op (metric|number)`, `parseGateText() → {conditions, unknown}`, `gateConditionsToText()`, `GATE_SYNTAX_HINT`); compiler `src/skills/skillCompiler.ts` passes each effect's conditions **straight through** (zero evaluation at compile time — evaluation still lives in exactly one file, `skillConditions.ts`, untouched); the input surface = one "🚪 发动门槛" card per effect (new `src/components/skillEditor/GateEditor.tsx`) **plus the Excel effect group growing to a 7th column "门槛"** (v1=3 / v2=6 / v3=7, `detectEffectGroupWidth` probes the header instead of counting, export is always 7 wide).
- **The most important design decision in this cut: a gate must never be silently misread.** `parseEffectGroup` no longer returns a bag of fields but `{fields, gateUnknown, orphanGate?, triggerUnreadable?}` — **every unreadable fragment is reported, none dropped**, and gate-text and trigger-text warnings share one channel (`parseWarnings`). After import the editor shows a **persistent** panel head: 「⚠ 有 N 处没看懂，这些内容没有被记下来（技能会照「没写」那样发动）」, dismissed only by an explicit click, while the input box echoes live "看懂了：A 且 B" versus "没看懂（这些条件不会生效）：「…」". Rationale: a gate is the thing that can **stop a skill firing** — a missed condition means the user believes there is a protection that is not there, which is the one input surface in this project that may not fake success.
- **"Choose one of" × gate = the whole group refuses to compile** (`CONDITION_CHOICE_UNSUPPORTED`): one choice definition has a single `conditions` slot, so per-effect gates are structurally inexpressible — the group is withheld and reported per line, rather than "freeing" the conditions that look unconditional (never invent gameplay). A lone effect still compiles as an independent definition with an unchanged id shape.
- **A real bug, found only because gate work made "my trigger won't save" the most common complaint**: the second input surface touched is `TriggerEditor`'s "照这个填" box, now sharing a **strict reader** `readTriggerCell(s)` with the Excel 触发 column — a string is accepted only if it **round-trips back to exactly itself**. The old `strToTrigger` matched only the main name, so 「受到伤害后→攻击」 was accepted with its subtype **silently discarded**, i.e. the user's "attack damage only" became "all damage" — the worst shape of "pretending to understand". Both the quick-fill box and the import path now use the strict reading, refuse cleanly, list every accepted spelling, and a test nails that **every dropdown option must pass it** (so the strictness can never lock out the editor's own vocabulary).
- **Honest leftovers, registered not fixed**: a gate written on a description-only effect gates nothing (the compiler still skips that effect as `NO_RUNTIME_PAYLOAD`, and no extra hint was added — the same fact said twice is noise); unreadable gate text is not archived, so re-export writes 「无」 there (the alternative witnesses are the per-line report at import time and the live red block; the user's own `.xlsx` always still holds the original). And **an effect group that contains only a gate creates no effect** — this replaced the original design ("a gate column alone counts as a group"), which in practice produced an effect with conditions and nothing to resolve; refusing and saying so won.
- **Tests 579/62 → 617/64**: new `skillGateText.test.ts` (14 — including the suspicion list of reversible wordings: 不少于/不超过, Chinese numerals, metric-on-both-sides, half-clauses landing in `unknown`, trailing quantifier tails 「数量/数目/数/量」 and filler 「为/是」 not blocking, global metrics ignoring a written subject) + new `skillExcelParsers.test.ts` (8) + `skillExcelFormat.test.ts` effect-group section rewritten for v3 with the strict reader + 5 compiler passthrough cases + **4 component-level cases** in `SkillEditor.runtime.test.tsx` + **1 mock-free real-engine closed loop** in `skillPipeline.test.ts` (gate text → `parseGateText` → a real `GameEngine`, two attacks: draw at hand=1, nothing at hand=2). That last case retires an evidence gap the reviewer named: `skillConditions.test.ts` still uses a `vi.mock` compile seam, which is **no longer necessary** once an input surface exists.
- **Five gates, re-run on the final tree**: `check` 0 errors / **617 tests, 64 files** / coverage snapshot **53.79 / 46.08 / 44.29 / 59.2** ≥ floors 42/34/34/47, exit 0 (floors untouched — the new files enter the denominator, which is real coverage growth, not a ratchet bump; a second snapshot on the same tree read 53.77/46.01/44.29/59.18, the known §12-22④ jitter) / lint 0 errors, 30 legacy warnings, zero new / build **1,989.10 kB, gzip 583.20 kB** (+13.32/+3.63 kB over v2.8.2 — the gate translator, the gate card, the strict reader and the 7th column).
- **Non-content hard anchor held**: ai-battle 300 games seed 1 → won=300, exhausted=0, VIOLATIONS=0, winners **{"1":112,"2":188}** verbatim against B10, per-faction ledger matching §12-43① cell for cell — and the independent reviewer's own 300-game run produced the identical reading, with two more verbatim runs on the final tree before registration.
- **Real-browser E2E, run on the post-review code**: live echo 「看懂了：手牌≤2 且 牌堆≥5」; an unparseable 「攻击范围内没有马」 flagged red while the card also says 「没有门槛」; choosing "one of" with a gate left shows the new red note; the strict reader refuses a half-written subtype and fills both dropdowns when written out; **an actual save** put `{metric:HAND_COUNT, op:LTE, value:2}` plus `trigger{onDamageTaken, attackDamage}` into the store; and a **26-column v3 workbook generated on the fly** was uploaded through the page's real `<input type=file>` — exactly 3 warnings named one by one (a half-written skill-level trigger / an orphan gate / a garbage gate) while the readable gate's two conditions stored fully and carried a runtime draw type, closing 导入→结构化→回显 and 读不懂→逐条清单 each on a real machine. **Disclosed as usual**: developer mode was opened through the DEV-only `window.__TK__` store hook (the passphrase is unknown to me by design and appears in no file; the hook writes the same boolean the Settings page writes, and is not a gameplay bypass), and afterwards the two edit keys were removed from localStorage, the page reloaded, fixtures deleted, the dev server killed and `:5173` re-checked as not listening.
- **Gate ③ (independent recompute) paid for itself**: a fresh session on the final tree built its own 20,160-clause parse matrix (0 semantic inversions, all 3,360 unreadable ones landing in `unknown`), 3,364 round-trips (0 conditions lost or mutated), and an out-of-repo engine probe (draw with the gate satisfied, no draw when unmet; a gated turn-end skill absent from candidates, forced activation answered `SKILL_CONDITION_UNMET`; choice-with-gate candidates 0, and 1 once the gate is removed). **3 blocking findings, all fixed** (a throwaway probe test left in the tree; the trigger subtype bug above; the invisible whole-group refusal in the editor) and **4 suggestions dispositioned line by line** (loosened parser + tests, global metrics no longer absorb a written subject, an amber note that `本次伤害` has no account to read outside damage triggers, and the missing component/closed-loop test layers above). One suggested fix was **declined with reasons**: archiving unreadable gate text would create a field that means nothing yet participates in rendering. **The costliest lesson of the cut is procedural**: two items in the interim review list referenced code that does not exist (a `gateConditions` field, a region of single-effect mode), each disproved by a throwaway probe before anything was touched, and the final report (3+4) is not the interim list ("5 blockers") — so the standing rule is **disprove every review finding against your own eyes before acting on it**; copying a review list blind breaks the code and buries the real bug under fake fixes.
- **GPT external review: not requested, with reasons** — the three discretionary calls (the grammar table, whole-group refusal for choice×gate, per-line unknown reporting) are all written down in §12-46 and §F reviewably, and gate ③ already demonstrated the second pair of eyes by catching a loosening bug.
- **Registered, awaiting the user's word (not debt)**: cost/payment column, follow-up effect column, value change + duration (the landing channel for derived panel values), damage-value modification, free target selection, zone targets + no-empty-cast, duel resolution, per-turn-once accounting, gates for "choose one of", and the plain-language vocabulary replacements (观顶/置牌入堆/剥离装备/被作用者) — **the last one is now delivered by [2.8.4]**; nine remain.
- **Two documentation falsehoods corrected while touching gates** (the *reverse* species: the doc says "cannot", the code already can): `skillConditions.test.ts`'s header and two case titles asserted "zero input surface in data layer/SkillEditor/Excel" and "the compiler cannot produce conditions today", and `dataTypes.ts`'s `choiceSource` comment pinned the Excel shape at "six columns". New discipline: **when a capability lands (or is deleted), grep its old "impossible" registration and rewrite it in the same cut**, or the next session reinvents it or mis-books the debt.

- **Remote CI verified**: GitHub Actions **CI #135** (run `36338619384`) **Success** on the docs commit `1715a1a`, total **4m 25s** — both matrix jobs (`test (22)`, `test (24)`) report **64 files / 617 tests passing** (read off the run summary's Vitest Test Report), `lint` 1m20s, `build` 53s, annotations only the 14 pre-existing legacy warnings (Node 20 deprecation ×2, React hooks ×12). Chain: feat `b56956c` (18 files, +1045/−105) → docs `1715a1a` → annotated tag `v2.8.3` on the registration commit. **Push needed the proxy this time**: a direct attempt failed with `Failed to connect to github.com:443 after 21066 ms` while `curl -sI https://github.com` returned 200 in the same moment (curl honors the system proxy, git does not), so master and the tag each went out through a **one-shot** `-c http.proxy=http://127.0.0.1:10808` push — no persistent git proxy config written. The backfill commit's own run was verified in-session too: **CI #136** (run `36339102114`) **Success** on `ac00813`, 3m 56s, same 64 files / 617 tests readings on both matrix jobs — registration closes at this layer.

## [2.8.2] - 2026-09-27

2.8 cut 3 — **dead-paradigm cleanup + the remote-play double track put on the books** (a *non-content* cut: hard anchor = B10 verbatim). Nine files from the project's first architecture generation — "one record per card, an importer loads it into a registry" — were deleted after being proven unreachable; the multiplayer skeleton that shares their zero-consumer status was deliberately **kept**, and the ruling that separates the two lists is now written down.

- **How they surfaced**: the user asked how general data is recorded (one file per card, like a card-game simulator, or one central ledger?). Answering required grepping the whole reference surface, which also produced a dead-code list — and the user then said "if they really are unused, clear them out".
- **Deleted (nine files)**: `src/importer/{GeneralImporter,SkillImporter,PackageImporter,types}.ts`, `src/data/registries/{GeneralRegistry,CardRegistry}.ts`, `src/data/examples/caoCao.json`, `src/skills/dataSkillExamples.ts`, plus `src/data/types.ts` — the last one is a **second-tier orphan**, not directly dead: its three interfaces (`GeneralData`/`CardData`/`SkillDataReference`) were consumed only by the two registries being deleted, so a one-pass cleanup had to follow the chain. `src/data/` is now the pure ledger pair (`generals.ts` + `cards.ts`); official content stays one `createGeneral(...)` line per general, player edits stay read-time localStorage overlays, and the source file is never rewritten.
- **Deliberately kept**: `network/`, `room/`, `server/`, `session/` (e.g. `WebSocketServer.broadcast()` is a pass-through stub) and `skills/SkillDataRegistry.ts` (a Map-style registry never instantiated) are equally unreferenced. **Zero references is not sufficient grounds for deletion** — you must also ask whether the file is groundwork for an approved future need. These are, so deleting them now and re-adding them later would be pure churn.
- **Two documentation falsehoods corrected in passing** (both of the "the doc claims more than the code does" family): `PROJECT_ARCH_MAP` §B listed `components/xlsxSecureReader` as a module — **that source file never existed**, only `xlsxSecureReader.test.ts` (the SheetJS read-path guard), a 2.2.11 transcription error; and both ARCH_MAP and three places in `AGENTS.md` said Excel import flows through `importer/`, when the real surface is `components/SkillEditor.tsx` + `skills/skillExcelFormat.ts` (read = SheetJS, write = exceljs). New discipline: grep a module's importers before trusting any "feature X lives in module Y" claim in the AI layer.
- **New fact fished out while scoping multiplayer**: `EngineState.players[].generalPool` today holds **whole `General` objects** (`setup/runtimeSetup.ts:31` types it `General[]`, `store/gameStore.ts:228` writes the drafted objects, `gameStateAdapter.ts:85` passes them through, `StateSerializer` `structuredClone`s them) — so a cross-machine snapshot already carries card definitions as a **side effect**. Since `hand`/`generalPool`/`fieldGenerals`/`deck` are deliberately data-agnostic `unknown[]`, no test pins "a snapshot must be self-sufficient to render one card", and one presentation-field trim would blank the remote screen for free. The future cut's job is therefore **to promote the free ride into a contract** (define the minimal render surface + a regression nail proving a guest with an empty local pool still renders every card), not to build a second delivery channel. ARCH_MAP §B's stale claim that D-1/D-2 must close before the network layer can be activated was fixed — both closed long ago (2.2.21 / 2.2.25).
- **Registered, not implemented** (`PROJECT_ARCH_MAP` §G item 11 + `PROJECT_HANDOFF` §13 items 5-6, per the user's decisions of 2026-09-27): ① **host authority** ("whoever opens the room is the house": the host's machine resolves the game, guests send actions and receive state snapshots, so a DIY pool only needs to exist on the host) travels with the remote-play line; ② **content packages** — **corrected the same day at the user's instruction**: this is *not* gated on room admission (my original "fingerprint mismatch ⇒ refused from the room" clause is **withdrawn as over-design**) and is **decoupled from multiplayer entirely**: an independent feature that exports local DIY generals to a package file carrying its own fingerprint and provenance, and validates + reports honestly at *install* time (never a silent half-install), useful on its own for backups, device migration and sharing creations. Its only prerequisite is the official/DIY discriminator (§G item 10) — without it there is no way to know what belongs in a package. Why both were needed at all (the user's stated reason, kept verbatim in intent): with ① alone, two players holding different home-made generals would still each lose a batch of them depending on who hosts. Multiplayer itself remains a new line of work, **not** folded into the 2.8 content sequence. **Registration discipline learned this cut: when booking a future need, record what the user actually said plus only the inferences they explicitly required — do not add "sensible-looking" gates on their behalf**, because that is how an independently buildable feature ends up blocked behind an unrelated one.
- **Verification**: `npm run check` 0 errors **after** the deletion — the compiler-level proof that nothing consumed these files; `npm run test` **579 tests / 62 files, zero change** (everything deleted was untested dead code); `npm run test:coverage` snapshot **52.28 / 43.99 / 43.41 / 57.68** ≥ floors 42/34/34/47, exit 0 — Stmts/Funcs/Lines rose and Branch dipped **only because nine never-covered files left the denominator**, so the ratchet floors stay put (a dead-code checkout, not a quality change); `npm run lint` 0 errors / 30 legacy warnings, zero new; `npm run build` = **1,975.78 kB / gzip 579.57 kB, the same reading as v2.8.1**. That last figure is this cut's witness: only imported modules enter the bundle, so deleting these nine files contributes **exactly zero bytes** (re-measured after the version bump, still identical) — a stronger claim than "all tests passed", which would only prove nobody noticed.
- **Non-content hard anchor held**: ai-battle 300 games seed 1 → winner seats **{"1":112,"2":188}**, won=300, exhausted=0, VIOLATIONS=0, **three consecutive same-seed runs identical**, per-faction ledger matching §12-43① cell for cell.
- **Remote CI verified**: GitHub Actions **CI #130** (run `36293221249`) **Success** on the docs commit `876bb2e`, total **3m54s** — both matrix jobs (`test (22)`, `test (24)`) report **62 files / 579 tests passing** (read off the run summary's Vitest Test Report, not inferred), `lint` 1m16s, `build` 54s, annotations only the pre-existing legacy warnings. Pushed **directly on the first attempt, no proxy**; `ls-remote` confirms `master` = `876bb2e` and `refs/tags/v2.8.2` is on the remote. The backfill commit's own run is verified in-session too: **CI #131** (run `36293549457`) **Success** on `f08669c`, 2m39s, again pushed directly.
- **Browser E2E: deliberately not run for this cut (declared, not silently waived)** — deletion only, no reachable UI/gameplay surface, bundle bytes unchanged, so there is nothing to click; unit evidence is not being dressed up as real-machine proof. Likewise the three-gate protocol's gate ① (layperson restatement) and gate ③ (independent recompute) do not trigger, because this is not a gameplay cut; the substitute witnesses are named above. **GPT external review: skipped, with reasons** — the deletion set is uniquely determined by grep + `tsc` evidence with nothing to adjudicate, the double track is the user's own product decision rather than our discretion, and the one judgement call (keeping `network/` etc.) is written down with its criterion.

## [2.8.1] - 2026-09-27

2.8 mainline **cut 2 — the developer-mode credential stops being readable (刀 A0, touches `src/`, a *non-content* cut: baseline anchor B10 must hold verbatim) plus the chained "if/then" paper contract (刀 A, zero `src/`)**. Triggered by the user's new requirement: now that generals carry an *identity*, developer mode's main job is to mark a general edit/creation as official vs DIY — so its passphrase must not be readable by anyone.

- **Audit findings before writing code**: the passphrase lived in **plaintext** in `src/store/gameStoreEditorActions.ts` and `src/components/Settings.tsx`, and shipped into `dist/index.html` (two hits) — anyone opening the offline single file and viewing source had it. A second, dead password-free bypass (`setDeveloperMode`) existed with **zero callers repo-wide** but was still reachable from the console, which made the passphrase decorative. Git history has carried the plaintext since the very first commit.
- **Adjudication: history is *not* rewritten** (blast radius = 193 commits + every tag + the remote + CI, unacceptable). The consequence is stated plainly: **the old passphrase is permanently burned** — anyone who can read the repository can recover it — which is exactly why rotation was mandatory rather than cosmetic. The new passphrase exists only as a SHA-256 digest constant and is written into **no** document, commit, test or memory entry.
- **New single credential surface** `src/domain/devGate.ts`: `DEV_MODE_DIGEST` (64 hex characters, no plaintext anywhere in the tree), `digestOfSecret()` via WebCrypto, `isSecureDigestAvailable()`, `matchesDeveloperModeDigest()` which **normalizes case before shape-testing**. Fail-closed: if `crypto.subtle` is unavailable the UI says the environment is unsupported instead of ever degrading to a plaintext comparison (no second decision path), and error copy never echoes what was typed.
- **Store**: `setDeveloperMode` deleted (this cut *removes* a state-transition path, it adds none); the toggle splits into `enableDeveloperMode(digest): boolean` — synchronous, sets the flag only on an exact digest match — and `disableDeveloperMode(): void`. Leaving developer mode asks for nothing: it only takes a capability away (user's ruling: entering needs the passphrase, exiting does not). Asynchronous work stays in `Settings.tsx`, so the engine and store keep zero async churn.
- **Tests (+12 → 579 / 62 files)**: `domain/devGate.test.ts` (7 cases, **`// @vitest-environment node`** because jsdom has no SubtleCrypto — first encounter of this pit) covering a known SHA-256 vector, determinism, the constant's shape, rejection of null/empty/63-char/non-hex input, uppercase-digest acceptance after normalization, and a real-chain wrong-passphrase rejection; `store/gameStoreEditorActions.devGate.test.ts` (5 cases, jsdom, fed precomputed digests only) covering rejection with state byte-unchanged, acceptance, credential-free exit, and a structural nail that the bypass keys are gone from the store surface. **The tests caught a real logic bug**: the shape regex ran *before* case-normalization, so uppercase digests were rejected — invisible while the constant was an all-zeros placeholder, exposed the moment the real digest was filled in. No test file contains a passphrase.
- **Artifact and browser verification**: `dist/index.html` and `src/` both score **0 hits** for any passphrase fragment while the digest appears exactly once. Real-browser E2E in two environments — dev server (a secure context) and the **built single file over `file://`**: a wrong passphrase yields 「密码错误」 and the row still reads 未开启; the correct one opens and reveals the dev-only editor/drill entries; exit needs no credential; **a page reload returns to the inactive state** (developer mode is in-memory only, which also corrects HANDOFF §12-34③'s stale "persisted" claim); `window.__TK__` is `undefined` in the production bundle, so the dev hook is not a console bypass there.
- **Threat model, stated in the file header**: this gate defends against *casual reading and accidental edits*, not against local tampering — an offline single-file app hands its code to the user, and no client-side passphrase is access control.
- **刀 A (paper only, zero `src/`)**: `PROJECT_ARCH_MAP.md` §F gains the **chained "if/then" composition contract table** across the standing twelve axes plus three appendices (failure/interruption semantics, choice-inside-a-chain prohibition, live-example column) and a re-anchoring notice — registered as **zero implementation, zero triggerable instance**, explicitly not "chained semantics are available". §G item 1 is corrected against line-level evidence (~~pure UI/Excel work~~ → the engine already evaluates conditions, the **data layer has no field for them**, so scope = four sites: data layer, compiler mapping, editor UI, Excel shape); execution order ①→②→④→③→⑤→⑧ is unchanged. New §G item 10 records that **no official/DIY discriminator exists** (the only DIY trace repo-wide is the `DIY_IDENTITY` weak proxy) and states 刀 E's precondition: edits persist to localStorage while developer mode does not survive a reload, so "who made this change" needs an on-disk marker or official edits keep applying after developer mode is off.
- **AGENTS.md**: the standing-rules section folds in the **three gates** a gameplay cut must pass (layperson restatement → build → independent recompute, the third never waived), as the user directed — riding this docs commit, no separate commit, no separate CI run.
- **Verification:** check 0 errors; **579 tests / 62 files**; coverage snapshot **52.13 / 44.04 / 43.01 / 57.51** (post-version-bump re-run 52.1 / 43.84 / 42.95 / 57.47 — §12-22④ jitter snapshot policy, floors 42/34/34/47 all passed, ratchet not raised); lint 0 errors / 30 legacy warnings, zero new; build single-file **1,975.78 kB / gzip 579.57 kB** (new v2.8.1 baseline; 1,975.17/579.23 retired). **Non-content anchor: B10 verbatim** — ai-battle 300 games seed 1 → **{"1":112,"2":188}**, won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, two same-seed runs byte-identical apart from timing lines, per-faction ledger matching §12-43① in full. **CI status: verified green** — **CI #127** (run **36268545526**) **Success** on the docs commit `aa63c09`, total 4m22s; `test (22)` and `test (24)` each report **62 files / 579 tests passed** (read from the run summary's Vitest Test Report), `lint` 1m24s, `build` 50s, annotations only the legacy Node.js 20-deprecation / React-hook warnings. Push went **directly** (no proxy); remote re-checked via `ls-remote` (master = `aa63c09`, tag `v2.8.1` present). The backfill commit's own run was verified in-session too = **CI #128** (run **36268877011**) **Success**, 3m54s, on `7f03e21` — registration stops at this layer. **The independent-recompute gate (the third of the three gates AGENTS.md now records) was actually run**: a second session re-executed all five gates on the final tree (same readings: 579/62, build 1,975.78/579.57 kB), re-ran the anchor twice (`{"1":112,"2":188}`, VIOLATIONS=0, byte-identical apart from timing lines, per-faction ledger matching line by line) and spot-checked three code facts (the password-free action survives only as a test assertion of its absence; digest comparison normalizes before shape-testing; no `persist`/`partialize` anywhere, so developer mode really is in-memory).

## [2.8.0] - 2026-09-27

2.8 mainline **cut 1 — general identity lock (a *content* cut: the standard-mode AI direct pool is locked too, so the baseline anchor moves B9→B10 — per user decision D3, rolling the AI-side lock back to preserve B9 is forbidden)**: cards with the same identity are dealt once per room and the deal itself is the lock.

- **Six-rule spec, contract-first** (`PROJECT_ARCH_MAP.md` §F identity-lock table written before code): ① same-name generals share one *identity*; identity × faction = one concrete card type. ② **dealt = locked** — once a candidate card reaches a seat it is that seat's, and a card never drafted still **sinks** the identity for the rest of the room. ③ within one seat the main surface and the 群 surface dodge each other's identities; 群 same-name is room-global; two *different* players holding the same name under *different* factions stays legal. ④ the lock is independent of survival — death/discard/return never releases it — and there is **no retroactive rewrite**: old saves where same-identity cards coexist only show a warning, while a *new* conflict at assembly time **rejects the whole batch with an explicit reason**. ⑤ identity `'DIY'` never locks; a **blank identity means no identity, no lock**; ⑥ same lock keys dedupe inside one seat's surface too.
- **Data layer**: `General.identity?: string`, resolvable **only** through new `src/domain/identity.ts` (fallback chain: undefined → the general's own name; blank → null/no lock; `DIY_IDENTITY` exempt; lock key = `(identity, faction)`). Legacy localStorage needs zero migration; Excel keeps its pinned six columns (decision D1 — no seventh column).
- **Draft chain** (`buildDraftCandidates` gains the dealt ledger + editor-merged pool params): all lock filters sit **before** each shuffle and consume zero randomness; the store tracks a `draftDistributed` ledger of everything dealt to earlier seats (drafted or sunk) — a B-class presentation mirror verified **not** to enter canonical `EngineState`.
- **AI direct pool** (`ai/matchSetup`): same lock on the seeded path; explicit seat lineups that collide throw `身份锁拒绝整批装配：…` instead of silently filtering (red line: validators may reject, never invent a second state path).
- **Editor**: new 「🪪 身份管理」 registry panel (create / rename-with-cascade / delete-refused-with-referrers; official derived identities cannot be deleted) persisted under `three_kingdoms_identity_registry`; per-general identity dropdown (default = own name / no-identity·unlocked / registry entry / ＋create-new); skill-editor attribute grid widened to match.
- **Draft screen**: conflicting legacy cards render greyed with a ⚠ badge and a red banner stating the coexistence is display-only, never rewritten; selection of a conflicting card is refused.
- **Battle-window bypass (D3)**: five lock switches + identity whitelist encoded in the `#ai-battle` hash (`lock=off,share,multi,noexp,global` + `lockwl=…`); defaults are *all follow the lock*; bypasses live only in the drill window — never in `EngineState`, never in replays, never in the standard room-creation chain. Contract-matrix errata registered honestly: the planned "allow same identity across factions" switch is a no-op in this context, so it ships as its **inverse stress mode** (global same-identity exclusion); candidate-count coverage reuses the existing `poolPerPlayer`.
- **Honest no-op disclosure**: the official 95 generals contain **zero same-faction same-name pairs** (the 4 same-name pairs are all cross-faction 魏↔晋), so "same-faction uniqueness" is provable but currently has no official instance — positive tests run on synthetic pools with injected identities; the B10 numbers corroborate (per-faction seat/winner counts identical to B9).
- **Baseline B10 (re-anchored)**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **{"1":112,"2":188}**, won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, two same-seed runs byte-identical; per-faction ledger in HANDOFF §12-43①. B9 `{"1":117,"2":183}` stays as the historical anchor (§12-33①/35).

## [2.7.4] - 2026-09-26

2.7 mainline **cut 5 — convergence verification, the closing cut (a *non-content* cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: 2.7's nine-item proposal is settled by adjudication and paper, not by new machinery. The cut asks three questions — does the equipment-loss event semantics we froze still hold, is the "free-combination building blocks" grammar actually expressible in what the engine already has, and does an external reviewer agree — and answers all three without touching runtime code.

- **Equipment-loss semantics adjudicated three ways (the only `src/` change is one negative test)**: `EQUIP_STRIP` → `applyEquipStripEvent` pops from the head of `armorCards`, so it **does** derive `CARD_LOST{via:'EQUIP'}` (deliberately without `remainingHand`); armor **destroyed while absorbing damage** (`AttackResolver` precomputes `destroyedArmorCardIds`, consumed in `damageEvents.ts:104-110`) does **not** derive; a dead general's surviving armor **buried with the field entry** (`damageEvents.ts:130-133`, defeated branch clears `survivorArmor`) does **not** derive. Three stated reasons: demand-driven (no skill yet asks for the other two), widening the event source means replacing the baseline anchor B9→B10 which is a content cut's prerogative, and the derivation point must stay single (`enqueueDerivedConsequences`) because the cost of each extra source is asymmetric. A construction sketch for a future widening is filed instead of code (`via` value pool grows to three, `cardFilter==='equipment'` becomes a set test, re-entry re-assessed).
- **The criterion, written the right way (采纳 GPT 2.7 fourth-review Q3)**: an event describes *"does canonical machinery need to know this causal fact"*, **not** *"did something physically disappear"* — so `CARD_LOST` must **not** be generalized into "all equipment leaving play", and any future flip requires a **new semantic demand**, never symmetry aesthetics; the `via` field carries its causal origin in its own name. Wording changed, behaviour unchanged. `src/core/transitionEquivalence.test.ts` gains the nail: both non-deriving paths emit zero `CARD_LOST`/`CARD_GAINED` and 枭姬 standing on the field stays silent.
- **Nine-slot building-block grammar table, on paper (承接池① first step, `PROJECT_ARCH_MAP.md` §F)**: current form is a **single-layer product** — *timing × sub-filter × (AND gate) × subject ×〔choice, at most one〕effect (parameters)*; seven of nine slots are closed and two (count/cooldown, chained if-then) are **structurally empty**, not "pending". Cost order: ① gate input surface (engine side already ready from 2.7.3 — pure UI/Excel work) → ② candidate-domain/subject-axis widening (still capability layer) → ③ count/cooldown (a **new canonical fact**: must enter `EngineState` to stay replay-reproducible, so it amends the state contract, not the compiler) → ④ chained "if/then" (changes the compilation and grouping model = **the largest single one; table before code**). ① and ② are independent; ③ and ④ are each their own cut.
- **Reading constraint adopted (fourth-review Q2)**: a grammar slot ≠ an implementation primitive — the two layers are read separately, and this table is **not** a licence to add a runtime mechanism per slot. Cost in particular is **an independent syntactic slot but not an independent Effect primitive** at this stage; should it ever ship inside effect parameters, the table must still name its **position and settlement order** (when paid, what if payment fails, before or after choice, whether it emits events, whether the skill can still be cancelled), because hiding it entirely lets a gap that is "not a word in the grammar" come back in another shape. The table's value is the **middle contract between grammar and UI**, not a UI spec.
- **Compilation-time sub-filter truth, established by grep (not by assumption)**: the compiler folds **exactly three** filter fields (`damageTypeFilter` / `turnSubType` / `cardFilter`); `deploySubType` exists only on the input surface (three places: `generals.ts:62`, `TriggerEditor.tsx:46`, `skillExcelFormat.ts:56,98`) and **never reaches compilation**; `killSubType:'killAlly'` and `turnSubType:'otherTurn'` are skipped at compile time as `TRIGGER_SUBTYPE_UNSUPPORTED` (`skillCompiler.ts:174-190`); `expireCondition` has **no** runtime consumer. Corollary: the data-layer `cardSubType:'equipmentLost'` (an umbrella term) vs the compiled `cardFilter:'equipment'` reading `via==='EQUIP'` (`SkillTriggerBridge.ts:143`) is an **intentional narrowing**, i.e. the source of B-grade fidelity.
- **2.8 proposal + execution order adopted (fourth-review Q4)**: the nine items stand, but the sequence becomes **① → ② → ④ → ③ → ⑤ → ⑧** (the grouping/grammar layer before the canonical-state layer), with ⑥⑦⑨ not escalated, plus a three-sentence acceptance line — a cut that only *expresses an existing engine capability* may go first; a cut that *adds canonical facts* must first prove a real content demand; a cut that *changes the grammar's combination model* must settle its contract before UI or content. Fourth review does **not** block this closing cut. Full Q&A archived outside the repo at `GPT_DISCUSSION_2_7_4_REPLY.md` (2,834 chars; normalized reconciliation `(len,hash) = 2507/2285867755` identical on both sides; typography is hand-laid and not part of the reconciliation).
- **Zero new vocabulary, zero content**: no new events, Effect primitives stay at **9**, trigger keys at **10**, canonical actions at **13**, data-layer trigger words at **22** (10 supported + 12 on-demand pool), Excel v2 at six columns; `generals.ts` untouched, ledger stays at **40 definitions / 129 honest skips**, `battleReport` at 39 rows. Structural evidence for "no runtime change": the single-file build is **byte-for-byte the same size as 2.7.3**.
- **Tests (+1 → 532 / 56 files)**: the two-way equipment-exit negative pair above lives in one case; no other test moved.
- **Non-content hard anchor achieved**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **{"1":117,"2":183} verbatim vs B9** (won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, same-seed runs byte-identical after stripping timing lines, all five per-faction anchors matched). **Browser E2E deliberately not run this cut** — the only `src/` edit is a test file, gameplay is untouched and the build is byte-identical; no unit test is passed off as a live match.

## [2.7.3] - 2026-09-26

2.7 mainline **cut 4 — skill gate conditions, the "am I allowed to ring at all" primitive (a *non-content* cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: skills can now ask whether the position is eligible before they fire, and the answer is a pure lookup that changes nothing. The twelve-cell contract table landed in `PROJECT_ARCH_MAP.md` §F first; this entry is that table turned into code.

- **The contract, pinned**: a condition is a **pure predicate over already-recorded facts** — zero events, zero state writes, zero RNG — evaluated **after** the identity check (identity first, threshold second). Six metrics (`HAND_COUNT` / `GENERAL_HP` / `ARMOR_POINTS` / `FIELD_GENERAL_COUNT` / `DECK_COUNT` / `EVENT_VALUE`), five operators (`LT/LTE/EQ/GTE/GT`), three subjects (`SELF` default / `TARGET` / `ATTACKER`), right-hand side = constant `value` **or** `compareTo{metric,subject?}` (compareTo wins), array = AND, **fail-closed** (any unresolvable metric makes the whole gate unsatisfied — refusing to fire is honest, firing on an unverifiable premise would invent gameplay), and `undefined`/`[]` means behaviour is byte-for-byte what it was. Conditions never enter the replay and never enter `EngineState`.
- **One implementation, three consumers (new `src/skills/skillConditions.ts`)**: ① the trigger path — `SkillTriggerBridge.buildCondition` folds its old switch into an `identityCheck` and returns `identityCheck(context) && evaluateSkillConditions(...)`, so **all ten trigger keys share one gate with zero per-key code**; ② the decision path — `turnEndSkills.listTurnEndSkillCandidates` filters with the same predicate, so the turn-end ask window, `legalActions` and the AI driver see one identical notion of eligibility (`listAllTurnEndDefinitions` deliberately stays unfiltered = honest disclosure surface); ③ `TurnEndSkillResolver` **re-evaluates independently** and answers with a new rejection reason `SKILL_CONDITION_UNMET` rather than silently settling a no-op skill.
- **Fifth "wired ≠ configurable" inoculation (§12-41①)**: `skillCompiler.singleDefinition()` and the choice-group definition are built **field by field with no spread**, so the compiler can never emit `conditions` — and since the decision-path consumers re-derive from `EngineState`, the real-machine path where a built-in skill carries its own gate is **unreachable in a zero-content cut** (same structural fact as §12-40①). Proof therefore rides the **compile seam** (`vi.mock('./skillCompiler')` + a `vi.hoisted` probe targeting `general.id==='sc_actor'`), while the trigger path still uses the only real entry point `engine.registerPlayerSkills`. Stated plainly, no unit test passed off as a live match.
- **Zero new vocabulary, zero content**: no new events, Effect primitives stay at **9**, trigger keys at **10**, canonical actions at **13**; `generals.ts` carries no payload and none of the 168 built-ins has a `conditions` field; SkillEditor and the Excel v2 columns have no input surface; the runtime ledger stays at 40 definitions / 129 honest skips and `battleReport` at 39 rows. State, action, validation and UI layers are untouched.
- **Tests (+22 → 531 / 56 files, new `src/skills/skillConditions.test.ts`)**: 10 pure-evaluation (all six metrics, three subjects, operator boundaries `[false,true,true,true,false]`, compareTo precedence, AND/empty, the full fail-closed spectrum including unknown metric / NaN / Infinity / unknown operator, plus a purity nail asserting `JSON.stringify(state)` is identical across a call); 6 real-engine trigger-path (a closed gate emits zero events without freezing the table, the gate touches no facts, `TARGET` never rings on `TURN_START`, `EVENT_VALUE` reads a real attack's damage, the gate sits **before** the offer so an unmet threshold does not even open `CHOICE_REQUIRED`, and an omitted `conditions` vs an explicit `undefined` produce byte-identical event streams and end states); 6 decision-path (candidate and `legalActions` drop together while the disclosure list still shows it, the resolver refuses independently and books nothing, earlier rejection reasons are not masked, a satisfied gate opens the ledger exactly like an ungated skill, four-way agreement, and a twin general compiled without the probe yields `conditions === undefined`).
- **Hotseat browser E2E** (single dev instance on 5199, killed afterwards and re-checked via netstat, nothing save/download clicked): the gate evaluator ran 18 checks against the **live `EngineState`** (real readouts — hand 5, deck 46, field 0 → after a genuine `DEPLOY_GENERAL` action 1 general / 1 HP / 0 armor; `TARGET`/`ATTACKER`/`EVENT_VALUE` honestly fail closed with no event); the live state JSON was byte-identical before and after and contains neither `"conditions"` nor any metric token; **real clicks on the HUD「⏭️ 结束回合」button twice** carried the turn across seats (turn 1→2→3, draw windows opened and closed normally); the live replay serialized to 8 entries / 247,005 chars with zero `conditions` and `header.gameVersion=2.7.3`; app console: **0 errors** (only the standing honest compiler diagnostic warn for 制衡 / 救援 `NO_RUNTIME_PAYLOAD`).

## [2.7.2] - 2026-09-26

2.7 mainline **cut 3 — choice producer minimal verification (a *non-content* cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: the either/or machine gets its two candidate generators — a **target picker** (generals on the field) and a **card picker** (the debtor's own hand) — **wiring only, zero content**. This is the minimal-verification knife the GPT third review pre-pinned (Q5) before choice may be expanded onto real producers: the question it answers is purely "can a producer legitimately generate a standard choice request?"

- **Zero new vocabulary**: no new events, Effect primitives stay at **9**, trigger keys at **10**, canonical actions at **13**, and the `PendingChoiceOption{label,events}` shape is untouched by a single character — so all four v2.6.3 consumers (validator / settlers / HUD / replay) required no changes at all. `generals.ts` gained no payload and none of the 168 built-in skills carries the new switch: structural guarantee behind the B9 anchor.
- **Two pure enumerators (new `src/skills/choiceCandidates.ts`)**: `enumerateTargetCandidates` walks players × fieldGenerals in array order, skips factions of dead players outright, scoped three ways (`ENEMY_FIELD` exclusive / `ALL_FIELD` / `SELF_FIELD`); `enumerateHandCardCandidates` lists the debtor's hand in array order as `{label, cardKeys:[id]}`. Both are deterministic — array order *is* the replay-reproducible order, zero randomness.
- **Compile-model-only fields**: `choiceSource?:'TARGET'|'HAND_CARD'` and `choiceTargetScope?` live on `DataSkillDefinition` and are reachable **only** through `engine.registerPlayerSkills` — the data-layer Skill type, SkillEditor and the six-column Excel format have no input surface (the fourth "wired ≠ configurable" prophylactic; a real producer reaching a real machine waits for a content cut that stands up the input surface on demand). Bridge dispatch happens in a private `buildChoiceOptions`: a producer-style definition is recognized only with **exactly one template effect** (multi-effect + choiceSource falls back to the v2.6.3 per-effect branch — pinned negatively), and **an empty candidate set sends no offer at all** (an open choice debt freezes the whole table; opening an empty one would deadlock it).
- **Hand selection centralized (new `src/core/eventProcessors/handSelection.ts`)**: `selectHandCards(hand, count, cardKeys?)` is now the single pick-point shared by the DISCARD / GIVE / DECK_PLACE settlers, with an additive optional `cardKeys` payload. Without it, head-slice and the `count=0` whole-hand sentinel stay byte-identical to v2.6.1; with it, cards are picked by runtime id keeping relative order (unknown ids ignored, count does not apply, empty array = same as absent). Every future "the player chooses which cards" primitive goes through this helper.
- **Tests (+13 → 509 / 55 files, new `src/skills/choiceProducers.test.ts`)**: TARGET 5 (open-account verbatim incl. per-option pre-translated DAMAGE payloads and deferred zero-commit; frozen-world double rejection + choosing option 1 settles only the chosen general 6→5 + RESOLVED + debt cleared; empty candidates = zero events and no freeze; multi-effect fallback; two byte-identical runs) · HAND_CARD 4 (labels + per-option cardKeys; choosing a non-head card settles exactly that one; empty hand = no offer; GIVE/DECK_PLACE cardKeys payloads verbatim) · pure-function 4 (three scopes incl. dead-player exclusion, enumerator shapes, six `selectHandCards` assertions, direct settler regression pins incl. deck order). Lesson pinned: the compile-model `SkillEffectData` has **no `id` field** (ids belong to the data layer) — writing one into test effects is TS2353×5.
- **Hotseat browser E2E, three promises kept** (single dev instance, killed and re-checked via netstat, nothing save/download clicked): ① the real enumerator reconciled against the live hotseat table (Zhou Tai / Xiahou Yuan, five hand cards, all three scopes); ② the offer seeded through the canonical `restoreEngineState` path (§12-23 doctrine); ③ a **real click on the HUD button「◈ 选项2：夏侯渊」** — only the chosen general dropped 4→3, the debt cleared, and the live replay's baseline document matched the post-choice store state byte-for-byte. Disclosed honestly: the decorative lobby→playing stretch rode the same canonical store actions the buttons themselves dispatch (background-tab timer throttling, §12-23), with real clicking reserved for the verified target; and `entries=0` in the replay is the *existing* open-document-only semantics of the first dispatch after a reset (§12-40②), so the criterion is initialState-equality, not entry count.

## [2.7.1] - 2026-09-26

2.7 mainline **cut 2 — event/state fact-contract governance (a *non-content* cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: the zustand compatibility mirror stops writing presentation vocabulary into fact fields. Item 2 of the GPT-third-review-calibrated nine-item proposal — it retires the §12-37② observation item that the 2.6 closing review escalated into a 2.7 governance line.

- **The defect**: `storeStateToEngineState` did two wrong things — `timelinePhase: String(store.turnPhase).toUpperCase()` produced dialect the engine never uses (`'MAIN'`, `'START'`, `'END'`), and `metadata` was **replaced wholesale** (only `{source, drawPlayerId/Reason/TotalCards}` survived; any canonical key, e.g. roomId, vanished). Worse than it looks, because `createEngineAwareSetter` (store/engineAwareSetter.ts:26) runs the mirror on **every display-only `set()`** — retitling, clearing a defeat banner, any pure-UI patch rewrites the engine state. The resident bridge then adopts that mirror output for the next dispatch (`engineExecutionBridge.ts:75-80`), so the dialect and the wiped metadata rode into live STATE_CHANGED snapshots and into what `liveReplayRecorder` reads off `afterState.metadata`.
- **Fix = invert the fold table, never upper-case it**: `applyEngineStateToStore` folds canonical `MENU/TURN_START → 'start'`, `DRAW → 'draw'`, `ACTION → 'main'`, `GAME_OVER → 'end'`, so `DISPLAY_TO_CANONICAL_TIMELINE` reverses exactly the three reversible ones. `'start'` is a **collapse bucket** — irrecoverable from the display value alone — so it is disambiguated by the previous canonical value (`TURN_START` else `MENU`); with no display signal at all the mirror **carries `previous.timelinePhase`** rather than guessing. `rebuildMetadata` now keeps canonical keys and layers the adapter's own observations (source marker + draw context) on top, resetting stale draw keys to null each pass.
- **Contract framing (D-9, now written up as ARCH_MAP C-4)**: `turn / consumedSkills / pendingChoice / rngState / metadata / timelinePhase` are **A-class game facts**; display fields are B-class presentation. A mirror may restore B into A's vocabulary but may not invent dialect — and since it emits **no events**, it can never become a second transition path (the standing red line, re-checked).
- **Behavior change, disclosed**: store-initial `timelinePhase` `'START' → 'MENU'`, in-play `'MAIN' → 'ACTION'`. Repo-wide there is exactly **one** legality reader of that field (`rules/ActionValidator.ts:44`, condition `!== 'ACTION' && phase !== 'playing'`, which does not block while playing) ⇒ match outcomes unchanged, backed by the B9 anchor.
- **Regression anchor = new file `src/store/engineFactContract.test.ts` (5 tests → 496 tests / 54 files)**: reverse-table four states; collapse bucket + carry-through + legacy-undefined shape; canonical metadata survival and stale-draw-key reset; `turn/rngState/consumedSkills/pendingChoice` passing through verbatim; and a **full-chain** case `engineAwareSetter → dispatchStoreAction → liveReplayRecorder`. Method note: contract changes like this must be tested through the whole chain — a pure unit test cannot prove "a display-only `set()` records nothing", which is the actual claim. Two real-semantics corrections came out of writing it: END_TURN legitimately advances `turn` 5→6 (it must not rewind to the display round), and `recordLiveDispatch` treats the **first** dispatch after a reset as document-start (no entry), so roomId is read at open time and the entry count moves on the second dispatch.
- **Hotseat browser E2E** (this cut touches the live-replay path, so it earns real-machine verification; single dev instance, killed afterwards and re-checked via netstat; nothing save/download was ever clicked): main menu → local game → room 定军山之战5610 → dice (throttling patch) → both seats drafted 10/10 → two draw rounds into ACTION. Three pieces of evidence — ① a real display-only store action (`clearDefeatEvent()`) fired during ACTION leaves `engineState.timelinePhase` at `'ACTION'` (pre-fix it wrote `'MAIN'`) and replay entries stay **4→4** (nothing recorded); ② a real ⏭️ end-turn click then takes turn 1→2, canonical `'DRAW'`, entries 4→5 (the mirror did not corrupt the next dispatch); ③ the live document (5 entries, header `gameVersion:'2.7.1'`) rebuilt through `ReplayPlayer` gives **processed=5, overrideFailures=[]**, and with metadata excluded the final state is **byte-identical (9848 chars)** with both sides at `'DRAW'` ⇒ **the MAIN→ACTION fold inside the §12-37③ reconciliation recipe is retired as of this cut**.
- **New finding, deliberately not fixed (→ §12-39, 2.8 candidate)**: on the real store room-creation path the live replay `roomId` is **always `'local'`** — the room name only ever lives in the display field `store.roomName`, and the sole writer of `metadata.roomId` repo-wide is `ai/matchSetup.ts:188` (the AI-battle tool line). So "the mirror loses roomId" is more precisely: the mirror loses whatever canonical metadata keys already exist (fixed here), and the store path simply never had that fact to lose. Fixing it would add a game fact, which needs real demand first.
- **Non-content hard anchor achieved**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **{"1":117,"2":183} verbatim vs B9** (won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, two same-seed runs byte-identical after stripping the two timing lines; all five per-faction anchors match §12-33①). ## [2.7.0] - 2026-09-26

2.7 mainline **cut 1 — battle-report keying (a *pure tool-surface*, non-content cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: the skill-trigger frequency report finally keys its rows properly — from the bare skill-name segment to **`将领模板id:技能名`** (general TEMPLATE id + skill name), resolved through a **cumulative alias table** built from both the pool and the field. Item 1 of the GPT-third-review-calibrated nine-item proposal. Zero gameplay paths touched: `trackSkillTriggers` stays default-off, so the B9 anchor is structurally guaranteed (and still re-measured twice).

- **The defect, proven before fixing**: the compiled skill id is `${ownerKey}:${skill.name}:${effectId}` where `ownerKey = runtimeGeneralId ?? general.id` (skillCompiler.ts:152) and `syncPlayerSkills` takes `getRuntimeCardId(general) || general.id` (runtimeIdentity.ts:12-15, instanceId wins). The owner segment therefore exists in **three assembly-unstable forms** — `<template>_p<seat>` (matchSetup.ts:138), `ai<seed>_c<n>` (matchSetup.ts:115), and `<template>__inst_<x>` (UI path). Measured: 60 games seed5000 `--skill-stats` under the old keying hit **0 configured rows** — nothing was matching; the key was simply the wrong one.
- **Fix**: `collectTemplateAliases(state, acc)` scans **both** `generalPool` and `fieldGenerals` (`entry.instanceId ?? general.instanceId` plus the copy id as dual keys), values = `templateOfCopyId(id) = id.replace(/_p\d+$/, '')`. It must be **cumulative**, not a per-step snapshot — a skill registration can outlive its roster entry (death-chain effects fire after the owner left the field), so a point-in-time snapshot would miss them. Unresolvable owners keep their **raw segment** so off-list rows are disclosed on the report rather than silently dropped. The old `snapshotFieldTemplates` is gone.
- **Expectation rows keyed the same way**: `configuredSkillRows()` now emits `{key: `${g.id}:${s.name}`, label: `${g.name}·${s.name}(${g.id})`}`; the CLI header reads 键=将领模板id:技能名. Side effect worth noting: **unique keys 38 → 39** (row count unchanged at 39) — 屯田 (魏邓艾 / 晋邓艾 `jin_007`) now counts into **two separate ledger lines**, which is the observation prerequisite for the same-faction-same-name ruling (content layer allows it, assembly layer must stay unique): you can only police uniqueness once the report can tell the two apart.
- **Re-measured (60 games seed5000)**: configured 39 · **triggered 10** · zero-trigger 29 — 贾充·帷幄(jin_004) 3; 杜预·拓略(jin_009) 2 / 羊祜·垦荒(jin_010) 2 / 司马炎·封赏(jin_014) 2 / 黄盖·苦肉(wu_004) 2; 邓艾·屯田(jin_007) 1 / 王元姬·英慧(jin_008) 1 / 文鸯·奋勇(jin_012) 1 / 周瑜·英姿(wu_005) 1 / 吴国太·补益(wu_021) 1. The synthetic `演練` rows correctly appear as off-list.
- **Tests re-shaped, none added (491 / 53 files, net zero)**: `skillTriggerKey` unit test rewritten for the alias-table contract (no table → raw `wei_001__inst_a:奸雄`; with table → `ai712_c3→wei_003:猛进`, `jin_004_p2→jin_004:帷幄`; no-colon fallback); the single-run case carries three **negative** pins (`not.toContain('__inst')` / `not.toMatch(/^ai\d+_c\d+$/)` / `not.toMatch(/_p\d+$/)`) and the positive shape pin moved into the 20-game `runBatch` roll-up — because a single `runMatch` can legitimately fire zero skill effects (seed 712 measured `KEYS []`), so a shape assertion on one game passes vacuously or fails falsely (§12-38②). Report tests now look template ids up via `idOf()` instead of hardcoding them.
- **Non-content hard anchor achieved**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **{"1":117,"2":183} verbatim vs B9** (won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, two post-redesign runs byte-identical after stripping timing lines; all five per-faction anchors match §12-33①).
- **Browser E2E: intentionally not run, disclosed** — zero `src/` runtime changes and no UI/interaction surface; the evidence is CLI-measured (seed5000 report) plus unit tests. Single unit tests are never presented as live proof.

## [2.6.3] - 2026-09-26

2.6 mainline **cut 4 — the choice player-decision channel, minimal loop (a *non-content* cut, hard anchor = B9 verbatim)**: the five-element contract from the GPT second-review Q4 (window → legal options → choice → result write-back → reproducible log) is landed end-to-end while **shipping zero payloads** — 观星/洛神/心战/自书/秘置 and the GIVE-target skills (遗计/好施) were explicitly **not** promoted on this knife's edge (Q4 forbids bundling), `generals.ts` untouched, ledger stays **40 definitions / 129 honest skips**, and zero built-ins use `effectMode` — the structural guarantee behind the B9 verbatim anchor.

- **Five elements, all canonical**: window = `EngineState.pendingChoice`, an **A-class slot** (rides the engine state into replay, projections and reconciliation — never a store-local flag); legal options = deterministic bridge pre-translation at trigger time (`CHOICE_REQUIRED{choiceKey, playerId, options:[{label, effects}]}`, zero RNG); choice = the **13th engine action** `CHOOSE_OPTION{choiceKey, optionIndex}`; write-back = `CHOICE_RESOLVED` clears the debt first (key-matched) and the selected branch then settles through the normal effect chain — **deferred settlement: nothing moves until the player commits**; reproducibility = everything A-class into the replay with zero `outcomeOverrides` extension. `choiceKey = ch:<turn>:<round>:<skillId>`; choice-mode definition ids end in `:choice` (no effectId — one skill, one decision window).
- **Frozen-world gates**: while a pendingChoice is live (and the game is not over), the `ActionValidator` rejects every non-CHOOSE_OPTION action with `CHOICE_PENDING`, and CHOOSE_OPTION from anyone but the debtor with `NOT_CHOICE_PLAYER` (**the debtor may act out of turn order — a decision is not hostage to the turn sequence**). `ChooseOptionResolver` carries six gates including `CHOICE_OPTION_OUT_OF_RANGE`. UI semantics: ending your turn with an open debt is an honest rejection (pick first, skip after); `commitEndTurn` rebuilds the projection off the rejected END_TURN and the turn counter does not move (test-proven and live-proven).
- **Compiler producer surface**: data-layer switch `effectMode:'choice'`; ≥2 runtime effects sharing one trigger signature compile into **one choice-mode definition**, lone effects compile independently as before; `listTurnEndSkillCandidates` enumerates definitions so a choice skill is a single candidate. Primitive count stays **9**, trigger keys stay exactly **10**.
- **`SkillEffectData` gains an optional `description`** (first source of the option label: `effect.description ?? skill.description ?? skill.name`). Honest ripple: compiled effect payloads now carry the field, so one strict `toEqual` in skillExcelFormat was updated; **event payload bytes are unaffected** (the bridge builds explicit fields — one structural witness for the B9 anchor).
- **New fact filed (§12-36②)**: **rejected actions also enter the replay document** (the D-1 SCRIPT precedent, extended) — `ReplayPlayer.play` reports processed = every action including rejections, so the rejection *reasons* themselves become four-path reconciliation material (the new transitionEquivalence case scripts CHOICE_PENDING + NOT_CHOICE_PLAYER inside one step sequence). Also registered: the pendingChoice is a **single slot** — simultaneous debts would collide; the frozen-world gate serializes them naturally today, and any real multi-window demand must amend the ARCH_MAP table first (§12-31① discipline, §12-36④).
- **Tests +13 → 479 / 52 files**: `ChooseOptionResolver.test.ts` new (7: six rejection gates, clear-then-settle ordering, selected-branch settlement, out-of-turn debtor allowed); `transitionEquivalence` +1 (择锋 closed loop across all four paths incl. the two frozen-world rejections, byte-equal reruns); `gameStore.pendingChoice.test.ts` new (3: turn-end-ask → activate → zero-card-commits debt → honest endTurn rejection → choose → thaw, plus manual-debt guard gates and projection-preserves-debt); `skillCompiler` net +2 (grouping / lone / label cases).
- **Non-content hard anchor achieved**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **{"1":117,"2":183} verbatim vs B9** (won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, same-seed double runs byte-identical after stripping timing lines; all five per-faction anchors match §12-33①).

Screenshot unavailable (`NATIVE_BROWSER_VIEWPORT_UNAVAILABLE`, hidden tab) → button innerText DOM-structure evidence used instead, disclosed honestly (§12-36⑤).
- **Verification:** check 0 errors; **479 tests / 52 files** (+13); coverage snapshots 50.1/42.22/41.12/55.61 above floors 42/34/34/47 (§12-22④, all four up vs 2.6.2, ratchet unchanged); lint 0 errors / 30 legacy warnings, zero new; build 1,956.38 kB / gzip 573.16 kB (+6.63/+1.69 kB vs 2.6.2 = choice event family + validator/resolver branches + compiler grouping + HUD text). package.json/lock = 2.6.3. **GPT judgment (per plan): skipped — the 2.6 third review stays pre-pinned to v2.6.4.** Next cut = v2.6.4 convergence-verification cut (reachability expansion + strategy-tier re-test of the narrow-chain skills + full hotseat game + replay verbatim + GPT third review + the 2.7 proposal, which must transcribe the two inherited questions: block-grammar table first, same-faction same-name policy). ## [2.5.1] - 2026-09-25

2.5 mainline, **cut 2 — the onDeploy wiring (a *non-content* cut)**: closes the registration-order gap recorded in §12-26 — a deploying general's own `onDeploy` listeners were registered only *after* the `GENERAL_DEPLOYED` event had already been dispatched, so deploy-moment skills could never hear their own arrival. Wiring ≠ promotion: 英慧/拓略/奋勇 stay descriptive until the v2.5.2 four-acceptance gate (GPT adoption ①).

- **Wiring (three points, no second state path)**: `TransitionContext` gains an optional `resyncSkills?(state)` hook — presence-gated, so hookless callers keep pre-wiring behavior verbatim. Inside `transition()`, after `ctx.processor.process(...)` and before the death-reentry loop, a step containing `GENERAL_DEPLOYED` with the hook present triggers `resyncSkills(next)` + `resolveTriggerChain` replay of that step; fresh (non-echo) events are appended and settleable ones (DEATH/TRIGGERED excluded) go through a **second call to the same EventProcessor**, so deploy-derived deaths fold into the existing bounded reentry loop — all settlement still flows through canonical machinery only. `GameEngine` supplies the hook as an arrow field calling `syncPlayerSkills` (type-only import both ways → no runtime cycle); dispatch passes `this`, so all four paths — resident bridge / reconcile rebuild / battleRunner fresh engine / ReplayPlayer — are covered by the single wiring point.
- **No double-fire**: every compiled definition pins `sourceGeneralId` and the bridge's `onDeploy` condition compares it against the deploying card id — the fresh listener did not exist during the first pass, and `TriggerEngine.register` is Map-keyed (`skill:${ownerId}:${skill.id}`), so re-registration is an idempotent overwrite.
- **Engine fact pinned by tests**: deploying sets field HP to the number of consumed cost cards (`currentHp = consumeCards.length`), so a 1-card deploy arrives at 1 HP.
- **Tests (net +1 → 432 / 49 files)**: the batch-2 negative activation probe (proving the gap) is replaced by two positives — (a) 英慧 synthetic payload fires **exactly once** on deploy and stays silent on the following END_TURN; (b) a deploy-chain byte-parity test (英慧 draw + synthetic onDeploy armor) runs the same script through resident / bridge / reconcile / replay paths and requires byte-identical event streams, asserting `{hp:1, armor:1}`.
- **Non-content hard anchor**: ai-battle 300 seed1 → **verbatim B5 {"1":117,"2":183}**, same-seed double runs byte-identical after stripping timing banners, won=300, VIOLATIONS=0, per-faction anchors unchanged. Byte-identity is a proof, not luck: no live compiled definition uses `onDeploy`, so the replay registry is empty and the wiring cannot have altered any game's outcome.
- **Ledger untouched**: 168 skills → **33 runtime definitions / 136 honest skips** (zero content change this cut).
- **Browser E2E: honestly skipped** — no player-perceivable gameplay touchpoint this cut; live trigger evidence folded into v2.5.2's promotion acceptance.

- **Guard tests** (`scripts/preflight-build.test.mjs`, new, 5 cases): each spawns a tmpdir copy of the script so the root-resolution semantics themselves are under test; `vitest.config.ts` include extended to `scripts/**/*.test.mjs` (coverage stays src-only, so these tests contribute no coverage lines). ## [2.3.2] - 2026-09-25

2.3 mainline, third cut (of the user-authorized five-cut plan): **P7 save-recovery real regression matrix — the integrity of users' actual saved games, flagged by the external reviewer as "cannot keep deferring"**. A *non-content* cut: the hard proof is ai-battle 300 seed1 staying byte-identical against baseline **B2 {1:114,2:186}** (two runs self-consistent, VIOLATIONS=0). What changes is only what happens on *bad* paths: crashes and silent save-loss become honest rejection + degradation.

- **First dedicated test file for the save/recovery chain** (`store/gameStoreRecovery.test.ts`, 16 cases covering the planned eight cells): normal-save full-field restore equality (turn/round/rngState/consumedSkills/room metadata/phase mapping) and blank-roomId explicit throw; legacy-shape fallbacks and out-of-range/missing version honest rejection with the store untouched; corrupt-JSON rejection + cleanup with a class-C console.error; missing-save reads null without clobbering other keys; room-mismatch keeps the archive for the caller to decide while double-saves follow latest-wins; autosave writes exactly what the engine just committed (read-back turn matches) and stays silent when off; a showSaveFilePicker probe pinned at 0 (game saves never touch system dialogs, v2.2.10 contract) and post-restore dispatch keeps working through the resident container; storage-API-denied degrades honestly (save=false / read=null / clear=quiet, turn chain unharmed, one warn per operation deduped via `__resetLocalGameSnapshotDiagnostics()`).
- **Blocking defect #1 found and fixed (validator defeated by bad data):** `isRestorableEngineState` mapped `players.map(p => p.id)` *before* the every-check, so a deep-corrupt save with a `null` entry crashed the recovery gate itself with a TypeError — red test proven, now playerIds derive only after validation passes; cell 3c pins it forever.
- **Blocking defect #2 found and fixed (storage failure could deadlock the turn):** autosave called raw `localStorage.setItem` inside `commitEndTurn` — private mode / quota / `file://` throws would break turn-end commits, a real user path for an offline single-file build. All three `localGameSnapshot` helpers now try/catch into honest degradation (save returns boolean, one console.warn per op, never rethrows); `GameBoard.saveGameSnapshot` consumes the result ("对局已保存"/"保存失败，请稍后再试") and **saveAndExit refuses to exit when the save failed** — otherwise "save and exit" would become silent save-loss.
- **Tests (+16 → 379 / 44 files).** Good-path semantics fully unchanged (cells 1/6 pin them byte-for-byte); the jsdom lesson that `localStorage` is a [LegacyOverrideBuiltIns] proxy (stub `Storage.prototype`, never defineProperty) is pinned in-file.

## [2.3.1] - 2026-09-25

2.3 mainline, second cut (of the user-authorized five-cut plan): **onTurnEnd + the turn-end ask window — the §4 frozen product rule finally lands, and D-3c's "the window itself may stay unrecorded; the game decisions made inside it may not" gets its first inspection under real evidence**. Like v2.3.0 this is a *content cut*: behavior changes by design, validated via same-seed two-run byte self-consistency + VIOLATIONS=0 + honest distribution disclosure; **baseline B2 {1:114,2:186}** is registered (v2.3.2+ non-content cuts must stay byte-identical against it).

- **onTurnEnd joins the game without ever auto-firing.** TURN_END is deliberately **not** mapped into `SkillTriggerBridge.TRIGGER_EVENT_MAP` (the three synced tables stay at 7): auto-firing on turn end would both rob the player of the decision and double-fire with the ask window. The one and only activation path is the canonical **`ACTIVATE_SKILL`** action `{skillId, generalId}` → new `TurnEndSkillResolver` (12th resolver) → emits `SKILL_ACTIVATED` (class A fact; new pure processor `core/eventProcessors/skillEvents.ts` appends the `EngineState.consumedSkills` ledger keyed `${turn}:${skillId}` — settlement lives exclusively in EventProcessor, the resolver only describes) plus effect events translated through the bridge's **static `createSkillEvents`** — the same code the trigger path uses, so no second effect mechanism and no second transition path. Legality is re-derived entirely from EngineState via `skills/turnEndSkills.ts` (`listAllTurnEndDefinitions`, unfiltered so rejection reasons stay honest: MALFORMED_PAYLOAD → PLAYER_NOT_FOUND → TURN_END_SKILL_NOT_FOUND → GENERAL_NOT_CONTROLLED → SKILL_ALREADY_ACTIVATED), which also makes the isLegal probe work on a cold engine with no registered triggers. Twelve-slot contract table registered in ARCH_MAP §F; this is the precedent for decision-type triggers (active* etc.) entering via canonical actions instead of event mappings.
- **The turn-end ask window = the first real business caller of the 2.2.25 entry, implemented as a store-layer pre-dispatch deferral.** A human, non-test-arena seat pressing 结束回合 while holding activatable onTurnEnd skills opens a reaction window (`rw:<turn>:<round>:turn-end-ask:<turn>:<playerId>:<seq>`) and the END_TURN dispatch is deferred behind store field `turnEndAsk` (class B: never recorded, and structurally unable to reach an archive — snapshots serialize engineState only). In-window activation and skipping are class A canonical actions that enter the replay; a double press of 结束回合 is an honest skip; AI seats and the test arena are ungated (the AI decides via **real** ACTIVATE_SKILL / endTurn actions through the driver and strategy layer — `legalActions` now enumerates turn-end skill candidates); with no candidates there is **zero behavior change**. GameBoard gains an amber ask banner (⚡ per-skill buttons + ⏭️ skip-and-end-turn), mutually exclusive with the generic window HUD.
- **Content closure with zero UI-plumbing cost:** PRACTICE_SKILLS gains「演練・守夜」(onTurnEnd draw 1, never auto-fires); editor/Excel support for the type was already free via `triggerTypeLabels` ("回合结束时") and the shared round-trip label table. Batch-filling runtime payloads for the 77 built-in generals stays out of scope (content production, separate kickoff).

2.3 mainline, first cut (of the user-authorized five-cut plan): **the content era opens — compile-honesty contract + onBecomingTarget as the first demand-driven trigger闭环, with the twelve-slot Trigger contract table landing as ARCH_MAP section F**. - **onBecomingTarget wired with zero new canonical events.** The trigger maps to the **existing** `BEFORE_DAMAGE` emission from AttackResolver's general-targeted branch (the base branch carries no `targetPlayerId`, so base attacks legitimately never fire; EventProcessor's default case for BEFORE_DAMAGE is a pure no-op notification, so non-skill paths keep zero side effects). Condition: owner matches `data.targetPlayerId` and, when declared, general matches `data.targetId ?? data.target`. Priority 50. Frozen timing: the derived counter DAMAGE queues at the trigger-chain BFS tail and settles **after** source DAMAGE + ATTACK_RESOLVED + AFTER_DAMAGE within one dispatch, and it still lands even if the target died from the source damage (events-generated-first semantics). Full twelve-slot contract table (Event/Timing/Source/Target/Trigger/Condition/Effect/RNG/Replay/Transition/Reentrancy/Death chain) registered in ARCH_MAP §F.
- **The only declared-but-silently-dead skill in the whole repo now lives:** PRACTICE_SKILLS「演練・回刺」(`ai/matchSetup.ts`) previously compiled to TRIGGER_UNSUPPORTED and was skipped without a trace; it now fires end to end. The B0→B1 per-faction stat delta is its live-fire evidence.

The per-entry granularity was forced by measurement: battleRunner mints a fresh engine every step, so full-set aggregation压出 707 warn lines in a 300-game run vs 92 at entry level; a browser window shows exactly 14. Event stream and gameplay behavior byte-unchanged by the diagnostics themselves. Editor/Excel UI consumption of the channel is registered as a future content need, not this cut.

Scope is deliberately the *entry mechanism only*: no skill or trigger type opens windows automatically — that content side stays gated on D-3's "wait for a project kickoff".

- **Container layer (`core/GameEngine.ts` + `triggers/ReactionWindow.ts`).** `openReactionWindow(event, participants?)` stamps a deterministic stableId into `ReactionWindow.open`: `rw:<turn>:<round>:<sourceEventKey>:<seq>` where the key is `event.data.stableId ?? event.type` and `seq` is a private per-resident-container counter — **rngState is not consumed** (a window's identity is not game randomness, so it must not occupy the random stream; §12-16e① closed here). `openedAt` keeps `Date.now` — observation only, harmless because windows never reach the replay document.
- **Bridge (`store/engineExecutionBridge.ts`).** Three new entries on the resident container: `openReactionWindowStore` / `passReactionStore` / `resetReactionWindowStore`. Window state lives only in the container and a store projection field — **never in EngineState** (a test pins `JSON.stringify(engine.snapshot())` byte-identical across open → pass → pass → close), so TransitionCore, replay and the four-way reconciliation are structurally untouched; this cut did not modify the pure transition path at all.
- **Store (`store/gameStore.ts` + `gameStoreTypes.ts`).** `reactionWindow: ReactionWindowState | null` plus actions `openReactionWindow` / `passReaction` (participants default to living players; an empty roster is an honest null no-op; the field clears itself when the last pass closes the window). `createRoom`/`resetGame` call `resetReactionWindowStore()` for new-match hygiene.
- **UI (`components/GameBoard.tsx`).** One sky-tinted HUD strip reusing existing primitives (under the skill-activation notices): "⏳ 反应窗口开启 · 等待通过" + window id + per-seat buttons (✓ once passed, click to pass otherwise). Zero DOM change while no window is open; no new component system.
- **Replay口径 (pinned this cut).** REACTION_WINDOW_OPENED/CLOSED are container-side EventBus emissions outside dispatch return streams, so they never enter `entry.events` — empirically confirmed in a live document (`mentionsReaction: false`). Reconstructing windows during playback awaits the trigger-content kickoff.
- **Multiplayer readiness:** the `open(event, participants:number[], stableId)` signature is already participant-list shaped; the local two-seat flow (open → both pass → auto-close) runs end to end. Network wiring remains dormant.
- **Disclosed change:** `ReactionWindow.open` gained a required third parameter — its only caller in the whole repo is the new GameEngine entry added here (the pre-existing "zero business callers" fact *was* the §12-9c gap).
- **Tests (+10 → 328 / 40 files).** New `triggers/reactionWindowEntry.test.ts`: deterministic id across two fresh builds (no clock in the identity segment), sourceEventKey from stableId with seq incrementing, default participants + dedupe, OPENED → partial pass → CLOSED exactly once with the right event order, stranger and post-close passes rejected, snapshot byte-unchanged, full bridge round trip, orphan pass after reset is a no-op, store actions exist and empty roster returns null.

## [2.2.24] - 2026-09-25

- **Legacy archives are never rewritten (D-4 core discipline).** A document without a header is read as schemaVersion 1 — honest dual read, same policy as 2.2.22. `ReplayRecorder.deserialize` now enforces an explicit three-state read口径: absent/1/2 → accepted, a *future* schemaVersion → rejected with `Unsupported replay schema version: N` instead of being silently misinterpreted. New static `schemaVersionOf(document)` is the口径 entry point.

But since 2.2.17 GAIN_ARMOR grants **point armor with no card instances**, and destroying that armor legitimately reaches the fallback branch — so plan-a ("delete once every caller supplies real instances") does not hold. Replays replay *actions*, not recorded events, so no legacy archive depends on the old shape either. The branch therefore **stays**, but its fabricated ids lose `Date.now()`: `legacy_armor_destroyed_<discardPile position + i>` — positional, fully deterministic, prefix unchanged so `ai/invariants.ts` keeps excluding synthetic armor cards from the card ledger.
- **Disclosed observable change:** synthesized placeholder-armor card ids changed shape (timestamp → position). Expected under the D-2 determinism program; draw sequences and outcomes are untouched — `ai-battle --games 300 --seed 1` stays byte-identical at {1:109,2:191} (won=300, VIOLATIONS=0).

- package.json/lock 2.2.23 → 2.2.24; `src/replay/index.ts` re-exports `ReplayHeader` / `REPLAY_SCHEMA_VERSION`.
